Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.

I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… definitely. Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.

And answering questions too, honestly. I’ll hear someone ask me something simple, and it’s like the words have to travel through mud before I can get them out. People ask me if I’m okay because I guess I’m moving weird, like… like I’m underwater or something.

It’s frustrating, because I used to be pretty quick on job sites. You kind of have to be, you know—tools, ladders, checking what’s hooked up where. Now I feel like I’m moving in slow motion.

I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I feel worn down more than anything.\n\nLike, on edge makes me think of when you’re waiting for a breaker to trip or a ladder to shift—you know, that tense kind of ready-for-something feeling. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there calmly making some big decision, it’s more like… when the pain’s bad and then I’m looking at bills, or thinking about how much trouble I’ve become for people, I get this thought that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. The pipes knock, the neighbors slam doors—old places are like that.\n\nBut voices talking to me, or seeing things that aren’t there… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.\n\nAnd answering questions too, honestly. I’ll hear someone ask me something simple, and it’s like the words have to travel through mud before I can get them out. People ask me if I’m okay because I guess I’m moving weird, like… like I’m underwater or something.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because I used to be pretty quick on job sites. You kind of have to be, you know—tools, ladders, checking what’s hooked up where. Now I feel like I’m moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale or anything, but my eating’s been weird. Like I’ll get through most of the day on coffee because the idea of food just kind of turns my stomach.\n\nThen later at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen, opening cabinets, picking at crackers or cereal or whatever’s there, not even really tasting it. It’s like when you’re on a job and you keep opening the toolbox even though you already know the part isn’t in there… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, my appetite’s all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. My thoughts feel like mine, I guess, even when they’re messy or going in circles.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll start thinking about something and end up on a whole other thing, like I’ll be trying to remember if I locked up a tool chest and then suddenly I’m thinking about this old foreman I had who used to yell about people misplacing drill bits… sorry. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.

Sometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.

So, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.

I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because most days I feel like I’m just sitting at the bottom of some dark hole. Like even if I’m around people I know, it doesn’t really reach me, you know? There’s this heavy, hopeless feeling I can’t shake.\n\nI almost didn’t come in—I kept thinking maybe I should just go to work and deal with it, because that’s what I usually do. I fix things all day, machines and wiring and whatever, but I can’t seem to fix this.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and two months, I think. It kind of started after that fall I had on an HVAC job, the one that messed up my back. That was in the fall, and after that everything just started coming apart. I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because most days I feel like I’m just sitting at the bottom of some dark hole. Like even if I’m around people I know, it doesn’t really reach me, you know? I fix things all day, machines and wiring and whatever, but I can’t seem to fix this.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and two months, I think. It kind of started after that fall I had on an HVAC job, the one that messed up my back. That was in the fall, and after that everything just started coming apart. I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. It’s not like I wake up and then at two o’clock it changes or anything I’ve really tracked.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because most days I feel like I’m just sitting at the bottom of some dark hole. Like even if I’m around people I know, it doesn’t really reach me, you know? There’s this heavy, hopeless feeling I can’t shake.\n\nI almost didn’t come in—I kept thinking maybe I should just go to work and deal with it, because that’s what I usually do. I fix things all day, machines and wiring and whatever, but I can’t seem to fix this.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and two months, I think. It kind of started after that fall I had on an HVAC job, the one that messed up my back. That was in the fall, and after that everything just started coming apart. I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I feel worn down more than anything.\n\nLike, on edge makes me think of when you’re waiting for a breaker to trip or a ladder to shift—you know, that tense kind of ready-for-something feeling. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there calmly making some big decision, it’s more like… when the pain’s bad and then I’m looking at bills, or thinking about how much trouble I’ve become for people, I get this thought that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. The pipes knock, the neighbors slam doors—old places are like that.\n\nBut voices talking to me, or seeing things that aren’t there… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.\n\nAnd answering questions too, honestly. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. Since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back… after that, everything just kind of started coming apart.\n\nI mean, I keep thinking if that day had gone different—there was this unit up on the roof, and the footing wasn’t right, or I don’t know, maybe I just wasn’t paying enough attention—but yeah. Since then it hasn’t really let up. It feels like it’s been there this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood or energy levels change throughout the day, or do they stay pretty consistent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. It’s not like I wake up and then at two o’clock it changes or anything I’ve really tracked.\n\nSome days I think maybe I’m a little more able to do things once I’ve been up for a while, but then other days it’s like… no, it’s just the same heavy feeling. And I get distracted by stuff, like if I’m trying to make coffee and notice the cabinet hinge is loose, I’ll stand there thinking about fixing it instead of actually doing anything.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern through the day. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because most days I feel like I’m just sitting at the bottom of some dark hole. Like even if I’m around people I know, it doesn’t really reach me, you know? There’s this heavy, hopeless feeling I can’t shake.\n\nI almost didn’t come in—I kept thinking maybe I should just go to work and deal with it, because that’s what I usually do. I fix things all day, machines and wiring and whatever, but I can’t seem to fix this.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and two months, I think. It kind of started after that fall I had on an HVAC job, the one that messed up my back. That was in the fall, and after that everything just started coming apart. I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I feel worn down more than anything.\n\nLike, on edge makes me think of when you’re waiting for a breaker to trip or a ladder to shift—you know, that tense kind of ready-for-something feeling. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there calmly making some big decision, it’s more like… when the pain’s bad and then I’m looking at bills, or thinking about how much trouble I’ve become for people, I get this thought that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. The pipes knock, the neighbors slam doors—old places are like that.\n\nBut voices talking to me, or seeing things that aren’t there… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. It feels like it’s been there this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood or energy levels change throughout the day, or do they stay pretty consistent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I feel worn down more than anything.\n\nLike, on edge makes me think of when you’re waiting for a breaker to trip or a ladder to shift—you know, that tense kind of ready-for-something feeling. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there calmly making some big decision, it’s more like… when the pain’s bad and then I’m looking at bills, or thinking about how much trouble I’ve become for people, I get this thought that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. The pipes knock, the neighbors slam doors—old places are like that.\n\nBut voices talking to me, or seeing things that aren’t there… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.\n\nAnd answering questions too, honestly. I’ll hear someone ask me something simple, and it’s like the words have to travel through mud before I can get them out. People ask me if I’m okay because I guess I’m moving weird, like… like I’m underwater or something.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because I used to be pretty quick on job sites. You kind of have to be, you know—tools, ladders, checking what’s hooked up where. Now I feel like I’m moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale or anything, but my eating’s been weird. Like I’ll get through most of the day on coffee because the idea of food just kind of turns my stomach.\n\nThen later at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen, opening cabinets, picking at crackers or cereal or whatever’s there, not even really tasting it. It’s like when you’re on a job and you keep opening the toolbox even though you already know the part isn’t in there… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, my appetite’s all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. My thoughts feel like mine, I guess, even when they’re messy or going in circles.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll start thinking about something and end up on a whole other thing, like I’ll be trying to remember if I locked up a tool chest and then suddenly I’m thinking about this old foreman I had who used to yell about people misplacing drill bits… sorry. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. Since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back… after that, everything just kind of started coming apart.\n\nI mean, I keep thinking if that day had gone different—there was this unit up on the roof, and the footing wasn’t right, or I don’t know, maybe I just wasn’t paying enough attention—but yeah. Since then it hasn’t really let up. It feels like it’s been there this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood or energy levels change throughout the day, or do they stay pretty consistent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. It’s not like I wake up and then at two o’clock it changes or anything I’ve really tracked.\n\nSome days I think maybe I’m a little more able to do things once I’ve been up for a while, but then other days it’s like… no, it’s just the same heavy feeling. And I get distracted by stuff, like if I’m trying to make coffee and notice the cabinet hinge is loose, I’ll stand there thinking about fixing it instead of actually doing anything.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern through the day. It’s mostly just there.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. The last few months sort of blur together, honestly. Some days I think, “Yeah, this is worse,” but then I remember I said that a while ago too, so I don’t know if it’s actually changing or if I’m just tired of it.\n\nIt’s like when you’ve got a noise in the house—like the fridge rattling or a bad bearing in a fan—and after a while you can’t tell if it got louder or if you just can’t stand hearing it anymore. So… I don’t have a good answer. I haven’t noticed a clear worse-or-better thing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where you felt significantly better for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might’ve been a day here or there where I got through things a little easier, but I wouldn’t call it “better for a while.”\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months since that fall at the HVAC job, when I hurt my back, and after that it’s like everything started unraveling. I keep thinking about that job site, too—how we were always rushing from one unit to the next, and somebody had left tools where they shouldn’t have, stuff like that—but anyway… no, I don’t think there’s been any real stretch where I felt significantly better.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.\n\nAnd answering questions too, honestly. I’ll hear someone ask me something simple, and it’s like the words have to travel through mud before I can get them out. People ask me if I’m okay because I guess I’m moving weird, like… like I’m underwater or something.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because I used to be pretty quick on job sites. You kind of have to be, you know—tools, ladders, checking what’s hooked up where. Now I feel like I’m moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale or anything, but my eating’s been weird. Like I’ll get through most of the day on coffee because the idea of food just kind of turns my stomach.\n\nThen later at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen, opening cabinets, picking at crackers or cereal or whatever’s there, not even really tasting it. It’s like when you’re on a job and you keep opening the toolbox even though you already know the part isn’t in there… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, my appetite’s all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. My thoughts feel like mine, I guess, even when they’re messy or going in circles.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll start thinking about something and end up on a whole other thing, like I’ll be trying to remember if I locked up a tool chest and then suddenly I’m thinking about this old foreman I had who used to yell about people misplacing drill bits… sorry. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. Since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back… after that, everything just kind of started coming apart.\n\nI mean, I keep thinking if that day had gone different—there was this unit up on the roof, and the footing wasn’t right, or I don’t know, maybe I just wasn’t paying enough attention—but yeah. Since then it hasn’t really let up. It feels like it’s been there this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood or energy levels change throughout the day, or do they stay pretty consistent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. It’s not like I wake up and then at two o’clock it changes or anything I’ve really tracked.\n\nSome days I think maybe I’m a little more able to do things once I’ve been up for a while, but then other days it’s like… no, it’s just the same heavy feeling. And I get distracted by stuff, like if I’m trying to make coffee and notice the cabinet hinge is loose, I’ll stand there thinking about fixing it instead of actually doing anything.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern through the day. It’s mostly just there.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. The last few months sort of blur together, honestly. Some days I think, “Yeah, this is worse,” but then I remember I said that a while ago too, so I don’t know if it’s actually changing or if I’m just tired of it.\n\nIt’s like when you’ve got a noise in the house—like the fridge rattling or a bad bearing in a fan—and after a while you can’t tell if it got louder or if you just can’t stand hearing it anymore. So… I don’t have a good answer. I haven’t noticed a clear worse-or-better thing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where you felt significantly better for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might’ve been a day here or there where I got through things a little easier, but I wouldn’t call it “better for a while.”\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months since that fall at the HVAC job, when I hurt my back, and after that it’s like everything started unraveling. I keep thinking about that job site, too—how we were always rushing from one unit to the next, and somebody had left tools where they shouldn’t have, stuff like that—but anyway… no, I don’t think there’s been any real stretch where I felt significantly better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where your symptoms seemed to clear up for at least a couple of weeks before returning?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever really cleared up like that. Maybe a day or two where I was distracted or had to deal with something else, but not a couple weeks.\n\nIt’s been going on pretty much since that fall at the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. About a year and two months now. After that, everything just kind of started unraveling, and I don’t remember any real break from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times where you felt depressed for a while, and then it went away for a bit, and then came back?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t feel like it went away and came back in chunks like that. It’s more like it’s just been there the whole time, about a year and two months now… since I fell on that HVAC job and messed up my back.\n\nAfter that, everything kind of started unraveling. I keep thinking if that day had gone different—like if I’d taken a different ladder or if the job hadn’t been so rushed—but… yeah, no, I don’t remember it lifting for a while and then returning. It’s just been ongoing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s just been ongoing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had times where you felt depressed for a distinct period, and then you felt completely back to normal for a while before feeling depressed again?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 49 / installation_maintenance_or_repair_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about a year and two months, ever since the fall at the HVAC job left me with the back injury and everything started unraveling.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not like completely back to normal. I don’t think I’ve had that. It’s just been going on for about a year and two months now, ever since I fell at that HVAC job and hurt my back.

I keep going back to that day in my head, like how everything was rushed and then after the injury it all just… unraveled. But no, I can’t point to a time where I was normal for a while and then depressed again. It’s been pretty steady.

And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. There’s this heavy, hopeless feeling I can’t shake.\n\nI almost didn’t come in—I kept thinking maybe I should just go to work and deal with it, because that’s what I usually do. I fix things all day, machines and wiring and whatever, but I can’t seem to fix this.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and two months, I think. It kind of started after that fall I had on an HVAC job, the one that messed up my back. That was in the fall, and after that everything just started coming apart. I kept thinking it was temporary, like once I got back on my feet things would settle, but… it didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to love hiking—there’s this trail I’d go to where the light comes through the trees just right—and painting, and messing around with projects at the makerspace. Even figuring out a tricky install used to feel kind of satisfying, like a puzzle.\n\nNow I’ll look at the paints or the tools, or I’ll see someone calling about going out or working on something, and I just… don’t answer. I stare at it and it feels like there’s no point even starting. None of it grabs me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Anxious, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I feel worn down more than anything.\n\nLike, on edge makes me think of when you’re waiting for a breaker to trip or a ladder to shift—you know, that tense kind of ready-for-something feeling. I used to get that on jobs sometimes, especially if someone was rushing me, but lately it’s more like I’m just… not really reacting much. I’m not sure if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there calmly making some big decision, it’s more like… when the pain’s bad and then I’m looking at bills, or thinking about how much trouble I’ve become for people, I get this thought that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes it goes further than that, like I catch myself thinking about ending it, and that scares me. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… sometimes when I’m exhausted I’ll think I heard my phone or someone outside, but then it’s nothing, and my building makes all kinds of noises anyway. The pipes knock, the neighbors slam doors—old places are like that.\n\nBut voices talking to me, or seeing things that aren’t there… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Everything is slow now. Like even just getting up from the couch to go to the kitchen, it feels like I have to talk myself through it.\n\nAnd answering questions too, honestly. I’ll hear someone ask me something simple, and it’s like the words have to travel through mud before I can get them out. People ask me if I’m okay because I guess I’m moving weird, like… like I’m underwater or something.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because I used to be pretty quick on job sites. You kind of have to be, you know—tools, ladders, checking what’s hooked up where. Now I feel like I’m moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale or anything, but my eating’s been weird. Like I’ll get through most of the day on coffee because the idea of food just kind of turns my stomach.\n\nThen later at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen, opening cabinets, picking at crackers or cereal or whatever’s there, not even really tasting it. It’s like when you’re on a job and you keep opening the toolbox even though you already know the part isn’t in there… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, my appetite’s all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. My thoughts feel like mine, I guess, even when they’re messy or going in circles.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll start thinking about something and end up on a whole other thing, like I’ll be trying to remember if I locked up a tool chest and then suddenly I’m thinking about this old foreman I had who used to yell about people misplacing drill bits… sorry. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. Since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back… after that, everything just kind of started coming apart.\n\nI mean, I keep thinking if that day had gone different—there was this unit up on the roof, and the footing wasn’t right, or I don’t know, maybe I just wasn’t paying enough attention—but yeah. Since then it hasn’t really let up. It feels like it’s been there this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood or energy levels change throughout the day, or do they stay pretty consistent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can pin it down like that. It’s not like I wake up and then at two o’clock it changes or anything I’ve really tracked.\n\nSome days I think maybe I’m a little more able to do things once I’ve been up for a while, but then other days it’s like… no, it’s just the same heavy feeling. And I get distracted by stuff, like if I’m trying to make coffee and notice the cabinet hinge is loose, I’ll stand there thinking about fixing it instead of actually doing anything.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern through the day. It’s mostly just there.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. The last few months sort of blur together, honestly. Some days I think, “Yeah, this is worse,” but then I remember I said that a while ago too, so I don’t know if it’s actually changing or if I’m just tired of it.\n\nIt’s like when you’ve got a noise in the house—like the fridge rattling or a bad bearing in a fan—and after a while you can’t tell if it got louder or if you just can’t stand hearing it anymore. So… I don’t have a good answer. I haven’t noticed a clear worse-or-better thing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where you felt significantly better for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might’ve been a day here or there where I got through things a little easier, but I wouldn’t call it “better for a while.”\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months since that fall at the HVAC job, when I hurt my back, and after that it’s like everything started unraveling. I keep thinking about that job site, too—how we were always rushing from one unit to the next, and somebody had left tools where they shouldn’t have, stuff like that—but anyway… no, I don’t think there’s been any real stretch where I felt significantly better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where your symptoms seemed to clear up for at least a couple of weeks before returning?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever really cleared up like that. Maybe a day or two where I was distracted or had to deal with something else, but not a couple weeks.\n\nIt’s been going on pretty much since that fall at the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. That’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I don’t feel like someone’s putting thoughts in my head or controlling my mind. At least I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worse in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Mornings are… I mean, they’re not good, but I don’t know if they’re worse than the rest of the day.\n\nSometimes I wake up and just lie there thinking about how I need to get moving, and then I’ll notice some stupid thing like the ceiling fan making that little clicking sound, and I’ll think I should fix it because I know exactly what’s wrong with it, probably just a loose screw or the bracket’s off a bit… but then I don’t.\n\nSo, yeah, mornings are hard, but I can’t really say they’re definitely worse than later. It all kind of runs together lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being tied to the seasons.\n\nI mean, winter’s always kind of a pain with the cold and getting up before the sun, especially when you’ve got to be somewhere and your hands are stiff before you even start working. And summer can be miserable too, crawling around in hot spaces, sweating through your shirt before lunch… but I don’t think my mood is like, worse every winter or better in spring or anything clear like that.\n\nIt just feels bad lately, not really like the weather flipped a switch.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been getting worse, better, or staying about the same over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on about a year and two months now, I guess. Since that fall on the HVAC job when I hurt my back… after that, everything just kind of started coming apart.\n\nI mean, I keep thinking if that day had gone different—there was this unit up on the roof, and the footing wasn’t right, or I don’t know, maybe I just wasn’t paying enough attention—but yeah. Since then it hasn’t really let up.
